  - rationale: Our naval counterattack has destroyed a Swedish Dreadnought and a Destroyer, shifting momentum. We must press the advantage with maximum naval, air, and nuclear focus to invade Swedish coastal cities (Stockholm, Turku, Espoo). Increase Nuke and UseNuke to 100/90 to prioritize nuclear missile development (Advanced Ballistics after Nuclear Fission completes in 4 turns) for strategic bombing. Naval and Air at 100 ensure sea control and air superiority. Maintain high Production, Gold, Science to fund military, and Happiness/Growth to support war economy. Diplomacy and Espionage secure city-state alliances and counter Swedish spies. All other flavors aligned with domination victory.

- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pocatello, leader of The Shoshone (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Shoshone
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Comanche Rider, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Yellow Brow, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Buffalo Pound, Replacing Well (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Unique Improvement: Barbarian Encampment (Can only be built on flat land tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Encampment.
          +15% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Any enemy unit which ends its turn next to this tile takes 5 damage (damage does not stack with other improvements).
          If within 2 tiles of an Encampment in Shoshone territory, all Shoshone units gain +20% Combat Strength.); Requires technology: Trade
        - Civilization Ability: Claim adjacent unowned land tiles of the same type when your borders expand, and up to 4 tiles when you gain a City. Can choose rewards from Ancient Ruins. Units gain +20% Combat Strength within 2 tiles of an owned Barbarian Encampment.
      - Leader: Pocatello
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Media Culture (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Media Culture: +25% Tourism and +1 Happiness generated by cities with a Broadcast Tower. +20% Culture from Stadiums.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Containment (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Containment: Trade Routes to City-States generate +2 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per owned City-State Trade Route (up to +10). +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 2 City-States originally in the World.
      - Space Procurements (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Space Procurements: May invest in Spaceship parts with Gold. Build Spaceship Factories in half the usual time. +20% Science from Research Labs.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Majesty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Specialists in Capital consume half the normal amount of Food.
        - * Palace Gardens built in Capital (+5 Food, +25% Great Person Rate, -2 Unhappiness from Urbanization, 1 Great Work of Writing slot, 1 Writer Slot).
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 3
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 9
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 3
        - NeutralBias: 2
        - FriendlyBias: 9
        - GuardedBias: 7
        - AfraidBias: 7
        - DiplomaticBalance: 7
        - Friendliness: 7
        - WorkWithWillingness: 10
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 7
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 9
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 1
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 5
        - MinorCivWarBias: 9
        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 1
        - Meanness: 9
        - Neediness: 6
        - Chattiness: 7
        - DeceptiveBias: 1
        - Rationale: Increase MinorCivWarBias from 7 to 9 to further incentivize attacks on city-states allied to Sweden, particularly Cahokia and Marrakech. Denying Sweden these allies weakens their influence and resources, complementing our naval and atomic offensive. Other persona values remain appropriate for our aggressive domination focus.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Advanced Ballistics
        - Rationale: Our current research (Nuclear Fission) will complete in 5 turns, unlocking Atomic Bombs and the Manhattan Project. We must immediately proceed to Advanced Ballistics to research Nuclear Missiles—the ultimate tool for breaking Swedish coastal defenses and cities (Stockholm, Turku, Espoo). Nuclear Missiles can destroy entire fleets and cripple city defenses, enabling our naval invasions. This technology path is essential for achieving military supremacy and accelerating our domination victory against Sweden.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Civilizing Mission (Policy)
        - Rationale: We are focused on domination and have many conquered cities. Civilizing Mission will help us retain buildings, gain gold on conquest, and increase production in puppeted cities, directly supporting our war effort against Sweden.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Ottomans have declared war on us and are a direct neighbor threat. Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI focuses military efforts on capturing their cities (Istanbul, Bursa) and rejects peace offers, securing our eastern flank and potentially gaining resources to boost happiness.
        - UpdatedTurn: 260
        
        ### Shanghai
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Events indicate we have made peace with Shanghai (city-state). Our previous hostile setting was for when we were at war. Now that we are at peace and Shanghai is neutral/protected by our allies, we should normalize relations to avoid unnecessary diplomatic friction and allow potential future cooperation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 290
        
        ### Ethiopia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Ethiopia has reopened hostilities (war declared on Turn 340). They are weak but distant. Setting maximum hostility ensures our AI defends against any attacks and considers opportunistic offensives to eliminate them if feasible, preventing distractions from the main war with Sweden.
        - UpdatedTurn: 350
        
        ### Sweden
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Sweden remains our primary enemy. Their naval counterattack caused losses, and they continue to defend Turku and Espoo. Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI focuses all military efforts on capturing Swedish cities and rejects any peace offers. We must destroy their navy and capture Stockholm to achieve domination.
        - UpdatedTurn: 373
        
        ### France
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: France is Sweden's vassal and at war with us. They are militarily weak but could distract forces. Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI treats them as a combatant and focuses on defeating them if they send forces, preventing distractions from the main Swedish front.
        - UpdatedTurn: 373
        
        ### Spain
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Spain is our loyal Freedom ally and fellow combatant against Sweden. Maximum relations ensure they remain absolutely committed to the war, diverting Swedish forces and preventing any diplomatic wavering. They are also our top World Congress ally and a key trade partner.
        - UpdatedTurn: 390
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: The Netherlands remains our crucial Freedom ally, trading partner, and neighbor. They are leading in diplomatic victory (109% votes) but are also at war with Sweden. Maintaining maximum relations ensures they continue diverting Swedish forces, trading resources (we just made a new deal for Coal/Gold), and do not betray us. Their military strength (15742) and city-state alliances make them a formidable ally we must keep absolutely friendly to secure our eastern flank and focus on Sweden.
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Rome is our loyal vassal with no hostile intentions. Normalizing relations (from 100/100) avoids unnecessary diplomatic strain and allows natural AI interaction. They are militarily weak (550 strength) and economically dependent on us. This change ensures our AI does not overcommit diplomatic resources to a subservient vassal, focusing instead on major threats (Sweden, France).
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Our naval and air war against Sweden is intensifying, with heavy losses on both sides (our Destroyer lost, their Submarine killed). We must dominate the seas to enable landings on the Swedish homeland (Stockholm, Turku). Increasing Nuke and Air flavors prepares for atomic and aerial bombardment of Swedish coastal cities and fleets. Naval, NavalRecon, and Mobile remain critical for sea control and amphibious assaults. Production, Gold, and Science support unit construction, upgrades, and technology for nuclear weapons. Maintaining high Offense, Mobilization, and MilitaryTraining ensures we can sustain and replace combat losses. High Diplomacy and Espionage secure our city-state alliances (Segou regained, Ecbatana and Kwa-Bulawayo friendly) and counter Swedish spies. Grand Strategy remains Conquest for domination victory.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors
        - Offense: 100
        - Defense: 80
        - Mobilization: 100
        - CityDefense: 80
        - MilitaryTraining: 90
        - Recon: 0
        - Ranged: 90
        - Mobile: 90

Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Swedish Sphere of Influence: Cahokia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Freedom:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

        You, Pocatello (leader of The Shoshone, Player 6), are making strategic decisions after turn 408.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pocatello, leader of The Shoshone (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Shoshone
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Comanche Rider, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Yellow Brow, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Buffalo Pound, Replacing Well (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Unique Improvement: Barbarian Encampment (Can only be built on flat land tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Encampment.
          +15% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Any enemy unit which ends its turn next to this tile takes 5 damage (damage does not stack with other improvements).
          If within 2 tiles of an Encampment in Shoshone territory, all Shoshone units gain +20% Combat Strength.); Requires technology: Trade
        - Civilization Ability: Claim adjacent unowned land tiles of the same type when your borders expand, and up to 4 tiles when you gain a City. Can choose rewards from Ancient Ruins. Units gain +20% Combat Strength within 2 tiles of an owned Barbarian Encampment.
      - Leader: Pocatello
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Media Culture (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Media Culture: +25% Tourism and +1 Happiness generated by cities with a Broadcast Tower. +20% Culture from Stadiums.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Containment (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Containment: Trade Routes to City-States generate +2 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per owned City-State Trade Route (up to +10). +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 2 City-States originally in the World.
      - Space Procurements (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Space Procurements: May invest in Spaceship parts with Gold. Build Spaceship Factories in half the usual time. +20% Science from Research Labs.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Majesty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Specialists in Capital consume half the normal amount of Food.
        - * Palace Gardens built in Capital (+5 Food, +25% Great Person Rate, -2 Unhappiness from Urbanization, 1 Great Work of Writing slot, 1 Writer Slot).
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

Reduced Nuke from 95 to 40 - maintain minimal deterrence capability without building an excessive arsenal. Increased Diplomacy to 100 to aggressively compete for city-state alliances against The Netherlands, who is poised to win diplomatic victory with 46/42 delegates. All other priorities maintained: naval dominance critical for Sweden war and potential Netherlands conflict, science for Advanced Ballistics, and military buildup for conquest.
    - #1:

Nuclear Fission completes in 4 turns, so Advanced Ballistics should follow immediately.
    - #2:

      - args:
        - Policy: Civilizing Mission
        - Rationale: Civilizing Mission (Imperialism) directly benefits our 7 occupied cities with courthouses: retaining all buildings on conquest, gold on conquest, and +10-20% production toward buildings in puppeted/courthouse cities. This significantly improves the productivity of our conquered territories (Istanbul, Ankara, Rome, Antium, Cumae, Bursa, Edirne) which are currently running Infinite Research projects. The production bonus will accelerate building construction in these cities.

          - text: Let me analyze the current situation carefully:
            
            **Current State:**
            - Turn 408, Information Era
            - Playing as The Shoshone
            - Current research: Nuclear Fission (4 turns remaining)
            - Next research set: Advanced Ballistics
            - Next policy: Civilizing Mission
            
            **Victory Progress:**

            - The Netherlands has 2 (Amsterdam, Madrid) = 25%
            - Need 8 capitals total
            - Science: No progress from anyone except The Netherlands (Apollo complete)
            - Cultural: The Netherlands is the main contender with 4/5 influential civs. I have only 1 (Rome - Dominant)
            - Diplomatic: The Netherlands has 46 delegates (109% - already winning!), I have 16
            
            **Key Threats:**
            1. The Netherlands is about to win Diplomatic Victory (46 delegates, needs 42) - THIS IS URGENT
            2. The Netherlands is also close to Cultural Victory (4/5 influential)
            3. Sweden is my main military competitor with 3 capitals
            
            **Military Situation:**
            - I'm at war with Sweden and France
            - The Netherlands is my ally (Freedom ideology, +100 relations)
            - Spain is my ally (Freedom ideology, +100 relations)
            - Rome is my vassal
            
            **The BIG Problem:** The Netherlands has 46 delegates and needs 42 for Diplomatic Victory. The next World Congress vote is in 1 turn! This is extremely urgent.
            
            Wait, looking more carefully at the diplomatic victory section:
            - "Voting in 1 turn" - The Global Hegemony proposal is active
            - The Netherlands has 46 delegates, needs 42
            - This means The Netherlands WILL WIN the diplomatic victory next turn unless something changes!
            
            This is a critical emergency. The Netherlands is about to win the game via diplomatic victory.
            
            What can I do?
            1. I could try to change my relationship with The Netherlands to hostile and potentially go to war to disrupt their victory
            2. But war with The Netherlands would be devastating - they have 15,917 military strength vs my 9,528
            3. They have massive gold reserves (70,681 gold, 1,192 GPT)
            4. They control many city-states and have Spain as a vassal
            
            Actually wait - Spain is listed as my friend with DoF, but also as The Netherlands' vassal. Let me re-check...
            
            From the Players section:
            - Spain: "Our Vassal (Capitulated)" under The Netherlands
            - But Spain also has "Declaration of Friendship" with The Shoshone
            
            So Spain is a vassal of The Netherlands. That complicates things.
            
            The Netherlands has 46 delegates - more than the 42 needed. The vote is in 1 turn. This is essentially game over unless I can somehow reduce their delegate count or get more delegates myself.
            
            Wait, let me re-read the diplomatic victory section more carefully:
            - VotesNeeded: 42
            - The Netherlands: 46 delegates (109%)
            - Status: "World Congress - Voting in 1 turn"
            - Proposals include "Enact: Global Hegemony"
            
            If the Netherlands gets enough votes for Global Hegemony, they win. With 46 delegates, they could vote for themselves and get 46, which exceeds the 42 needed.
            
            Can I do anything about this? The vote happens in 1 turn. I can't stop it. The only thing I could potentially do is:
            
            1. Declare war on The Netherlands before the vote? That might disrupt their delegates from city-states if those city-states switch sides during war. But it's already "voting in 1 turn" so the delegates may already be locked.
            
            2. Actually, looking at the World Congress, the delegates are already counted. The Netherlands has 46. The vote is happening next turn.
            
            This seems like a desperate situation. The Netherlands is about to win diplomatically.
            
            But wait - I should also consider: maybe I can't stop the diplomatic victory. In that case, what's my best path?
            
            Actually, let me reconsider. The delegates shown are what each civ has. But for the Global Hegemony vote, each civ votes for someone. The Netherlands would vote for themselves (46), but other civs might not vote for them. 
            
            Actually in Vox Populi, for the Global Hegemony vote, each civilization votes. The Netherlands needs 42 total votes in their favor. They have 46 delegates of their own, so they can give themselves 46 votes, which exceeds 42. Unless there's some mechanic I'm missing, they win.
            
            The only way to prevent this would be if:
            - Some delegates are lost before the vote
            - War could cause city-state allies to flip
            
            But the vote is in 1 turn. Even if I declare war now, the delegates are likely already set.
            
            Actually, in Vox Populi, declaring war on a civ causes you to lose delegates from their city-states? No, it's the other way - if you're at war with a city-state's ally, you can't get those delegates.
            
            Hmm, let me think about this differently. The Netherlands has:
            - Base delegates from civs/city-states
            - Spain as vassal (Spain's 15 delegates probably go to Netherlands)
            - Multiple city-state allies
            
            If I declared war on The Netherlands, would their city-state alliances break? In Vox Populi, being at war with someone doesn't directly break their city-state alliances. But it could affect the voting.
            
            Actually, I think the diplomatic victory vote has already been calculated. The Netherlands has 46 delegates. The vote happens next turn. I probably can't stop it.
            
            Let me focus on what I CAN do:
            
            1. **Domination Victory**: I need to capture more capitals. I'm at war with Sweden (who holds Stockholm, Addis Ababa, Paris) and France (who holds Paris but France is a vassal of Sweden).
            
            Actually wait - Stockholm is Sweden's original capital. Addis Ababa was Ethiopia's capital (Ethiopia is defeated). Paris was France's capital (France is a vassal of Sweden). 
            
            For domination, I need to control or vassalize all original capitals. I currently control Istanbul (Ottoman), Rome, and Moson Kahni (my own). I need Amsterdam (Netherlands), Stockholm (Sweden), and Madrid (Spain - but Spain's capital is Seville? No, Madrid was Spain's original capital but it's occupied by Netherlands).
            
            Wait, looking at the domination victory section more carefully:
            - CapitalsNeeded: 8 (total original capitals in the game)
            - I control: Istanbul, Rome, Moson Kahni = 37%
            - Sweden controls: Stockholm, Addis Ababa, Paris = 37%
            - Netherlands controls: Amsterdam, Madrid = 25%
            
            So there are 8 original capitals: Moson Kahni, Istanbul, Rome, Stockholm, Addis Ababa, Paris, Amsterdam, Madrid
            
            I need to capture more capitals. I'm at war with Sweden and France. If I can capture Stockholm, Addis Ababa, and Paris from Sweden, that would give me 6/8 = 75%.
            
            But The Netherlands is about to win diplomatically. This is the priority threat.
            
            Actually, let me reconsider the timeline. The vote is "in 1 turn." If the Netherlands wins this vote, the game is over. But if no one gets enough votes, the top 2 get +5 delegates each.
            
            Hmm, but The Netherlands already has 46 vs 42 needed. They will win unless I can somehow reduce their delegate count or the vote fails.
            
            Actually, wait. Let me look at this again. The delegates listed:
            - The Netherlands: 46
            - The Shoshone: 16
            - Spain: 15
            - Sweden: 13
            - France: 6
            - Rome: 5
            
            If The Netherlands votes for themselves with 46, that's enough (need 42). Unless there's a mechanic where they need a majority of ALL delegates? Let me think...
            
            In Vox Populi, the World Leader vote requires a certain number of delegates. The Netherlands has 46 delegates out of a total of about 101 (46+16+15+13+6+5 = 101). They need 42. With 46, they exceed that threshold.
            
            So unless I can disrupt this before the vote... but it's happening next turn.
            
            I think the game might be essentially over unless I can somehow take action. Let me think about what actions I can take right now:
            
            1. Declare war on The Netherlands - this could potentially cause their city-state allies to not count? Actually, in Vox Populi, if you're at war with someone, their city-state allies still count for them. But declaring war on The Netherlands might cause them to lose delegates if... hmm.
            
            2. Actually, I just realized something. The Netherlands is allied with many city-states. If I could flip some city-states before the vote... but there's only 1 turn.
            
            3. I could try to propose something in the World Congress that would disrupt the vote, but the proposal is already set (Global Hegemony).
            
            This is really a desperate situation. The Netherlands is about to win.
            
            Let me focus on what I can actually control:
            
            **For domination**: I need to keep pushing militarily. I'm at war with Sweden and France. I should focus on capturing Swedish cities, particularly their capitals.
            
            **Naval situation**: I just had significant naval losses against Sweden (lost a Destroyer, Dreadnought, and Submarine). But I also killed several Swedish ships. I need to rebuild my navy.
            
            **Technology**: Nuclear Fission in 4 turns, then Advanced Ballistics. This gives me nuclear capabilities.
            
            **Policy**: Civilizing Mission is next - good for conquered cities.
            
            Let me think about the strategic priorities:
            
            1. **STOP THE NETHERLANDS FROM WINNING**: This is the #1 priority. If they win diplomatically next turn, everything else is moot.
            
            Can I declare war on The Netherlands? Looking at the relationship:
            - Public: +100, Private: +100
            - We're trade partners, open borders, same ideology
            - They're the strongest civ (15,917 military vs my 9,528)
            
            If I declare war, I'd lose all trade routes (major income), and face a much stronger opponent. But if they're about to win diplomatically, what choice do I have?
            
            Actually, wait. Let me re-read the diplomatic victory section more carefully:
            
            "Status: World Congress - Voting in 1 turn"
            
            This means the vote is scheduled for 1 turn from now. In Vox Populi, when you declare war on a civilization, does it affect their delegate count? 
            
            In Vox Populi, your delegates come from:
            1. Base delegates from being a civ
            2. Delegates from World Congress hosting
            3. Delegates from city-state alliances
            4. Delegates from ideology
            
            If I declare war on The Netherlands, their delegates shouldn't change immediately. However, if I could capture some of their city-state allies or liberate conquered cities... but there's only 1 turn.
            
            Hmm, actually, there might be another factor. Let me look at Spain's delegates. Spain has 15 delegates. Spain is a vassal of The Netherlands. In Vox Populi, a vassal's delegates typically vote with their master. So The Netherlands effectively controls 46 + 15 = 61 delegates? No, that's not right. Each civ controls their own delegates. Spain votes separately.
            
            For the Global Hegemony vote:
            - Netherlands (46 delegates) votes for Netherlands
            - Spain (15 delegates) as vassal likely votes for Netherlands
            - Sweden (13 delegates) votes for... probably not Netherlands
            - Me (16 delegates) - I vote for myself
            - France (6 delegates) as Sweden's vassal, votes for Sweden
            - Rome (5 delegates) as my vassal, votes for me
            
            If Netherlands gets 46 + 15 = 61 votes, that's way more than 42.
            If they get just their own 46, that's still more than 42.
            
            So The Netherlands will win the diplomatic victory next turn. I can't stop it.
            
            Unless... I declare war on The Netherlands, which might cause their vassal Spain to break free? No, that's not how it works. Vassals don't automatically break free when their master goes to war.
            
            Actually, in Vox Populi, if you declare war on someone, the vote still happens. The only way to prevent a diplomatic victory is to have enough delegates to vote against them or to reduce their delegate count below the threshold.
            
            Given that I can't stop the diplomatic victory... should I still try? What's the alternative?
            
            Actually, let me reconsider. Maybe I should focus on the domination path more aggressively. If I can quickly capture more capitals, maybe I can win before the diplomatic victory vote resolves.
            
            But the vote is in 1 turn. If The Netherlands wins the vote, the game ends immediately.
            
            Unless... the vote doesn't automatically grant victory? In Vox Populi, the Global Hegemony vote needs enough delegates to support a particular civ. If The Netherlands gets 42+ votes for themselves, they win. It's game over.
            
            So I'm in a truly desperate situation. Let me think about what declaring war on The Netherlands would do:
            
            1. Their delegates from city-states would... still count? Actually, I'm not sure if delegates are "locked in" before the vote or if they're calculated at the time of the vote.
            
            2. If I declare war on The Netherlands, and they're at war with me, their city-state allies that I'm also at war with might not provide delegates? Hmm, I don't think that's how it works.
            
            3. The key issue is: does declaring war affect delegate count? In standard Vox Populi, I don't think it does for the World Congress vote.
            
            OK, I think The Netherlands is going to win diplomatically very soon, and there may be nothing I can do about it. But let me try my best:
            
            The only thing I can think of is:
            - If I declare war on The Netherlands and capture some of their cities quickly, their delegates might decrease. But I can't capture cities in 1 turn.
            - If I could somehow get more delegates myself...
            
            Wait, I have 16 delegates. Spain has 15 and is a vassal of Netherlands. Rome has 5 and is my vassal. So I effectively have 16 + 5 = 21.
            
            The threshold is 42. I'm at 21. Even if I got all other delegates (13 + 6 + 15 = 34), I'd have 21 + 34 = 55. But that's impossible because France is Sweden's vassal and Spain is Netherlands' vassal.
            
            Hmm, what if I could flip Spain? Spain is listed as The Netherlands' vassal, but they also have a Declaration of Friendship with me. Could I liberate Spain somehow?
            
            I think the diplomatic victory is probably going to happen. But I should still make the best strategic decisions I can in case something unexpected happens (like the vote failing because other civs vote against Netherlands).
            
            Let me focus on what I should do strategically:
            
            1. **Technology**: Advanced Ballistics is set, which gives nuclear missiles and SS Boosters. But with Nuclear Fission still 4 turns away, I should consider if there's a better path. Actually, since I'm in the Information Era and need to maximize my chances, Advanced Ballistics is fine for nuclear deterrence and spaceship parts.
            
            Wait, but The Netherlands already completed Apollo. They might be going for science victory too. Let me check their science progress... they have 0 parts completed though. So they're not close on science.
            
            2. **Policy**: Civilizing Mission from Imperialism - this helps with conquered cities, retaining buildings and getting production bonuses. Good for my conquest strategy.
            
            3. **Grand Strategy**: Should I stay on Conquest or switch? Given the Netherlands' diplomatic victory threat, maybe I need to pivot...
            
            Actually, I realize I need to seriously consider: is there ANY way to stop the Netherlands' diplomatic victory?
            
            Let me count city-states and their allies:
            - Segou: My ally (1201 influence)
            - Kwa-Bulawayo: Netherlands' ally
            - Ecbatana: Netherlands' ally
            - Cahokia: Sweden's ally
            - Marrakech: Sweden's ally
            - Shanghai: Spain's ally
            - Ragusa: Netherlands' ally
            - Lisbon: Netherlands' ally
            - Vilnius: Netherlands' ally
            - Pago Pago: Netherlands' ally
            
            The Netherlands controls 6 city-states (Kwa-Bulawayo, Ecbatana, Ragusa, Lisbon, Vilnius, Pago Pago). Each city-state provides delegates.
            
            If I could flip even a couple of these... but I can't do that in 1 turn.

          - text: Let me analyze the current situation carefully:
            
            **Current State:**
            - Turn 408, playing as The Shoshone
            - Era: Information Age
            - At war with Sweden and France
            - Allies with Spain, friendly with The Netherlands, vassalized Rome
            - Currently researching Nuclear Fission (4 turns remaining)
            - 15 cities, 442 population
            - Military: 88 units, 9528 strength
            - Golden Age: 22 turns remaining
            
            **Victory Analysis:**
            - **Domination**: I control 3/8 capitals (37%), same as Sweden. Need 5 more capitals.
            - **Diplomatic**: The Netherlands has 46 delegates (109% of needed 42) - they could win diplomatically very soon! This is URGENT.
            - **Cultural**: The Netherlands is the contender with 4/5 influential civs. Very close to cultural victory.
            - **Science**: No one has completed Apollo yet except The Netherlands (Apollo complete, 0 parts).
            
            **Critical Threats:**
            1. The Netherlands is at 46 delegates vs 42 needed for diplomatic victory. They could win NEXT TURN at the World Congress vote!
            2. The Netherlands is 1 civ away from cultural victory (need 5 influential, have 4)
            3. The Netherlands has Apollo complete and could pursue science victory
            
            **The Netherlands is the biggest threat.** They have 109% of needed delegates for diplomatic victory. The Global Hegemony vote is happening in 1 turn! This is an existential crisis.
